# 559: Gengar – One Ghost, One Battalion!

"Ka-kaah~!"

Gengar immediately leapt out from its Poké Ball, standing beside Johan, black mist swirling around it. In its hands, the mist morphed into the shape of an assault rifle.

Rat-tat-tat—

It opened fire at Leon, who was still striking his signature pose.

Johan: "?"

Leon: "?"

But the crowd was utterly thrilled by this scene.

The smell of gunpowder was in the air—this was the real deal!

"Ahem!"

Another new referee, riding an Aegislash in Shield Forme, descended into the field.

"Both contestants, please prepare!"

His voice echoed throughout the stadium.

Thud! Thud!

On the giant screen, Leon and Johan's portraits appeared on opposite sides, with six Poké Balls each displayed intact.

Leon finally dropped his dramatic pose, his eyes blazing with excitement as he smiled at Johan.

"Teacher Johan! I finally get to battle you again! This time, I'll show you my full strength—let's both enjoy this to the fullest!"

Saying this, he grabbed a Poké Ball.

Johan smiled slightly, lowered his hat brim, spun his wrist, and also took out a Poké Ball, replying calmly:

"As you wish."

Rumble—

The ground trembled slightly as the battlefield shifted, revealing today's terrain for the match: Flat Ground!

"Contestants, summon your Pokémon!"

The referee shouted again.

Leon was first to throw his Poké Ball.

"Go, Cinderace!"

With a flash of red light, a rabbit-like Pokémon appeared, standing upright on two legs, sporting white ears and red fur on its head, its lower half resembling a soccer player's pants. It bounced lightly onto the field.

"Cinderace, the Striker Pokémon, Fire-type. Among all Pokémon, it boasts top-tier burst strength, jumping ability, kicking power, and balance. It can always land stunning, powerful shots—Rotom!"

The first Pokémon was Cinderace, huh?

As Cinderace landed, Johan's Poké Ball was thrown out too.

And appearing on the field—

Ceruledge!

"Goka!"

Ceruledge swung its twin blades lightly, and ghostly blue flames shimmered along the swords.

Cinderace's gaze sharpened, but it kept its easy smile, lightly bouncing on the spot, warming up for battle. Faint sparks flickered around its body, showing its serious attitude.

Both were Fire-types—

An inevitable clash of flames!

"That's Johan's Ceruledge! It can transform into Armarouge too!"

"Two Fire-types facing off—this is gonna be explosive!"

"And Leon's Cinderace is no joke either!"

The audience buzzed with opinions.

Unlike the casual spectators, top Trainers like Cynthia saw much deeper.

"Just judging by initial matchup, Teacher Johan is at a slight disadvantage," Lucian noted, pushing up his glasses.

Cynthia nodded.

Of course, that was only based on the surface; once the battle started, anything could happen depending on their tactics and execution.

"Battle—Start!"

The referee, still astride Aegislash, pulled back to a safe distance.

Leon stood smiling at Johan without making a move—he wasn't planning to take the initiative.

Honestly, throughout the entire tournament, Johan had never rushed an opponent.

But this was the first time an opponent deliberately offered him first move.

Johan understood why.

One reason: Leon was confident he could counter any first move.

Another...

Johan smiled slightly.

In that case, he would accept the "kindness."

"Ceruledge, Shadow Sneak!"

"Goka!"

Without hesitation, Ceruledge swung its blades. A shadow burst from under its feet, darting toward Cinderace at incredible speed.

Cinderace instantly sensed something odd—its Life Orb was acting up.

But at that moment, Leon's voice rang out.

"Cinderace, go in with Sucker Punch!"

Sucker Punch!

Cinderace's eyes sharpened, its body cloaked in a dark malicious aura, and it shot forward like a bullet toward Ceruledge.

If you looked carefully, the red tuft atop Cinderace's head had turned gray-black!

Its typing had changed—from Fire to Dark!

Libero Ability!

Libero: Before using a move, the Pokémon changes its type to match the move.

This unique ability allowed Cinderace to resist Ghost-type attacks (like Shadow Sneak) while its Dark-type move hit Ceruledge super effectively.

And importantly—

Thanks to Libero, Cinderace's Sucker Punch now gained a Same-Type Attack Bonus (STAB)!

This was why Leon had ceded first move.

This was why Cynthia and others thought Cinderace held the advantage.

But—

Johan knew all that too.

He still chose to move first because:

While Ceruledge lacked Ground-, Rock-, or Water-type moves to counter Fire-types,

it absolutely had moves to counter Dark-types!

BAM!!

Cinderace struck first, a Sucker Punch slamming into Ceruledge's chest.

The cursed armor cracked audibly, fine fissures spreading.

Weak Armor Activated!

Johan's eyes narrowed, hiding a slight grimace.

He commanded quickly:

"Close Combat!"

"Goka!"

Despite the painful hit, Ceruledge's speed had sharply risen thanks to Weak Armor.

Before Cinderace could stabilize, Ceruledge launched itself, both blades slicing fiercely.

Bam! Bam! Bam!—

A brutal Close Combat strike!

Cinderace tried to dodge but caught a slash across the shoulder, sending it flying!

However—

Weak Armor + Close Combat = MASSIVE Defense drop.

Ceruledge couldn't take another hit like that.

Thus, Johan pressed the attack!

"Close Combat again!"

Leon, realizing Ceruledge's vulnerability, wasn't backing down either.

"Cinderace, Sucker Punch again!"

Both Pokémon charged!

Johan's Aura merged with Ceruledge's spirit—

Under Aura's influence, Cinderace's lightning-fast Sucker Punch seemed slower.

Ceruledge remained calm.

Intercept!

BAM!!

Sword met Sucker Punch foot—

Blocked!

Leon's heart skipped a beat—he hadn't expected Ceruledge to block that.

Before Cinderace could recover from the surprise, Ceruledge's second blade landed a decisive hit.

Leon swiftly withdrew his Cinderace in red light.

"Return, Cinderace!"

Even with Libero advantage, he had nearly been outdueled!

The audience finally exhaled collectively.

That exchange had been so fast and intense, they hadn't even dared blink.

They barely understood it—

But it was thrilling!

Thanks to commentary from Raihan, who knew both Leon and Johan well, the crowd finally understood the tactics at play.

Peak Youth Masters battle!

Leon steadied himself.

This was Teacher Johan's true strength.

No room for underestimation.

Leon threw out his second Poké Ball.

"Go, Inteleon!"

Appearing was a slender, lizard-like Pokémon, posing dramatically like a secret agent wielding a gun.

"Inteleon, the Secret Agent Pokémon, Water-type. It hides special abilities within its body and can adapt its tactics on the fly—Rotom!"

Galar's Water starter—

Inteleon!

Seeing this, Johan immediately recalled Ceruledge.

With its defense now shredded, Ceruledge wasn't suited for more combat.

Moreover, Water-types could crush Fire-types.

But Ceruledge had already done its job—

Disrupted Libero's advantage and forced a switch!

Against Inteleon—

Johan chose:

"Go, Ka-kaah!"

"Ka-kaah~!"

From Johan's shadow, Gengar eagerly leapt out.

Wearing sunglasses, hands on hips, tongue sticking out,

Gengar struck a pose to challenge Inteleon's spy-like stance.

"Ka-kaah~(▼皿▼)σ"

The crowd erupted.

"Teacher Johan's Gengar!"

"How can a Gengar be so adorable?!"

"Gengar invincible!!"

Apparently provoked, Inteleon whipped out a monocle, snapping it onto one eye.

Item: Scope Lens!

More critical hits!

This confirmed Inteleon's ability:

Sniper!

Sniper: Boosts critical hit damage.

This time, Leon didn't hesitate—

he immediately attacked.

"Inteleon, Dark Pulse!"

Buzz—

Inteleon formed finger-guns, taking aim at Gengar.

The Scope Lens helped it lock onto two weak points:

Gengar's forehead and tongue.

Without hesitation, Inteleon fired two Dark Pulses like bullets.

Gengar just grinned wider.

It even glanced cheekily at Johan:

"Ka-kaah~!"

Heeey, this guy's cheating! Two shots in one move!

Johan just smiled.

Two shots?

Watch this.

"Gengar, Shadow Ball!"

Buzz—

Gengar floated up, black mist surging from it.

Two Shadow Balls appeared—

then a third—

then a fourth!

It hurled them to intercept the incoming Dark Pulses.

Boom! Boom!!

Mid-air collisions!

Inteleon blinked in shock.

Four Shadow Balls?!

At that moment—

Johan's trap snapped shut.

From every direction—

Left, right, above—

multiple Gengars emerged, each with twin Shadow Balls!

Inteleon realized—

Not just clones. Real attacks!

Multiple Gengars unleashed a barrage:

Shadow Ball storm!

Inteleon's eyes widened in horror.

Everywhere it looked: Shadow Balls raining down.

BOOM BOOM BOOM—

The battlefield was consumed in explosions and smoke.

The spectators were stunned.

How could a Gengar spam attacks while cloning itself?!

This wasn't a duel—

This was a one-Ghost artillery barrage!

Gengar, sticking out its tongue madly, gleefully shouted:

"Ka-kaah!"

(Ora ora ora ora!!)

You wanna strike poses?!

You think you can out-pose me?!

Only I, the great Gengar, can pose here!

Later, when Inteleon was interviewed post-battle,

it looked mournfully at the camera:

"Shoo."

Translation:

"I'm supposed to be the agent...

But that guy wasn't throwing Shadow Balls—

He was shelling me with an entire battalion!"

Gengar – One Ghost, One Battalion!

# 560: This Is the True "Wandering Dragon"!

"Huff—huff—"

Inteleon gasped heavily, staring at the merged Gengar, unable to hide the fear in its eyes.

Come on.

I just shot two blasts, and you rained down an entire arsenal on me?!

If it weren't for Protect combined with Substitute, and Inteleon's naturally agile body, allowing it to narrowly avoid Gengar's relentless bombardment, it would have been knocked out on the spot.

Even though it dodged most of it, Inteleon's condition was now worse than Cinderace's had been earlier.

Leon hadn't expected Johan's Gengar to have this level of burst power.

He had tried to research Johan's Gengar before.

But in previous battles, Gengar had only shown its sneaky ability to steal buffs and surprising physical attack prowess.

And most of the footage available was from League tournaments—not truly indicative of Johan's full strength.

No one knew Gengar could unleash such insane barrages.

Leon had already pulled out a Poké Ball, intending to swap Inteleon out.

After all, Inteleon was mainly brought in to counter Ceruledge.

But—

As he tried to recall Inteleon, the red beam was blocked by a dark force.

"Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar waved playfully at Inteleon.

"Don't go yet! We haven't even started playing~~"

Mean Look!

Inteleon: "..."

I don't wanna play with you!!

Leon realized swapping via Poké Ball was useless now.

"U-turn, Inteleon!"

Inteleon gritted its teeth, blue light flashing over its body as it darted at Gengar like a bullet.

U-turn would hit and then allow it to switch out, bypassing Mean Look.

A smart plan—but—

Facing the incoming Inteleon, Gengar simply grinned wider.

Hey!

"Thunderbolt, Gengar," Johan said calmly.

Gengar grabbed its own ears, stretching them into fake Pikachu ears—

ZAP!

Golden lightning surged across the battlefield with a sizzling sound, striking Inteleon head-on!

ZZZZZTTT—!!

Inteleon froze mid-dash, never even reaching Gengar.

That's why Leon had initially chosen to swap with the Poké Ball rather than risk U-turn.

Thunderbolt had nailed it.

Finally, Inteleon collapsed.

"Inteleon is unable to battle!" the referee declared.

The first Pokémon knocked out!

"Ka-kaah~ Ka-kaah~~"

Gengar, still disguised as a Pikachu, bounced around the field triumphantly.

"Pfft—this Gengar even had to cosplay as Pikachu to use Thunderbolt!"

"Confirmed—Gengar is a shapeshifting prankster!"

"Not gonna lie, that Thunderbolt was ridiculously powerful for a non-Electric Pokémon."

The audience roared with laughter.

Gengar's nature as a mischievous trickster was shining bright.

Meanwhile, among the newly arrived spectators—

In a corner of the stands, Professor Oak, Red, and Green had just sat down.

Red's Pikachu stood on his shoulder, head tilted, watching the field.

It scratched its head at Gengar's antics.

"That Thunderbolt... really strong," Red murmured.

His own Pikachu's Thunderbolt wasn't weak either.

But compared to Johan's Gengar...

And Gengar wasn't even an Electric-type!

This was the gap between them.

Green noticed Red's expression, feeling conflicted.

Was Red still thinking about challenging Johan after seeing this?

But when he met Red's eyes—

He was stunned.

Red wasn't discouraged at all.

In fact, his fighting spirit was burning even brighter.

He was focusing even more intently on the match.

"Red..."

Green opened his mouth but said nothing.

Professor Oak, meanwhile, smiled warmly at both boys.

"You did great, Inteleon."

Leon spoke softly to the Poké Ball, then turned toward Gengar, still bouncing excitedly.

After a brief thought, he threw his third Poké Ball.

"Go, Dragapult!"

The stealth dragon—Dragapult!

Triangular head shaped like a jet, four hollow holes atop its skull, housing tiny Dreepy, a slender body, and a translucent ghostly tail.

"Dragapult, the Stealth Pokémon, Dragon/Ghost-type. A pseudo-legendary Pokémon of Galar—Rotom!"

A pseudo-legendary Dragon!

Johan knew Dragapult well.

In earlier competitive scenes, Dragapult had been a top-tier pick.

The holes in its head housed two Dreepy, used both as weapons and companions—

a special symbiosis unique to Dragapult.

In games, this mainly affected certain moves.

But in the real world...

Catching one Dragapult meant catching THREE pseudo-legendaries!

Johan couldn't help but think: there goes another fortune.

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar tilted its head at Dragapult.

Hey, this guy feels kinda like my little "electronic pet"...

Gengar's instincts were sharp.

But Dragapult—

It stared at Gengar with caution.

It sensed... a terrifying pressure from Gengar.

A dangerous aura.

One of Johan's Poké Balls began to tremble at his waist.

Noticing it, Johan calmly ordered:

"Gengar, return."

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar looked confused.

I can still fight, boss!

But obediently floated back anyway.

In front of outsiders, the family's Gengars still had to show respect.

Once Gengar returned, the trembling Poké Ball opened by itself.

"Bwooo~~"

Bursting onto the field—

Dragonite!

Excited, energetic—Dragonite was ready to GO!

Leon immediately responded.

"Dragapult, Dragon Dance!"

"Baruto—!"

Dragapult floated higher, pulsing with a rhythmic energy.

Johan raised an eyebrow and quickly countered:

"Dragonite, Dragon Dance!"

"Bwooo—!!"

WHOOSH!

Dragonite's massive wings flapped hard, propelling its large body upward at shocking speed.

Both dragons, dancing in the air, boosting their Attack and Speed simultaneously.

A certain dragon: "You're ALL MINE—GAH!"

"Dragapult, Dragon Darts!"

Swoosh swoosh—

From Dragapult's head holes, two Dreepy were launched like missiles, cutting through the air toward Dragonite.

Dragon Darts—a signature move only Dragapult could use.

"Dragonite, Dragon Rush!"

"Bwooo!!"

Dragonite's body surged with draconic energy, forming a massive dragon aura, charging at Dragapult.

The Dreepy arrows tried to intercept—

but Dragonite powered through, barreling straight at Dragapult.

The clash neared.

"Tail Whip, Dragapult!"

Leon commanded.

Dragapult's tail stiffened, shimmering green.

Dragon Tail—

a move that could forcibly switch out an opponent's Pokémon.

It had been Leon's key tactic in the original series: disrupting opponents' rhythms.

Against impulsive Trainers like Ash, it worked decently.

But against strategists like Johan—

Johan had already anticipated it.

"Extreme Speed!"

he shouted.

BOOM!

Dragonite's body blurred—

and instead of colliding head-on, it instantly flashed behind Dragapult.

"Whoa! Johan's classic prediction!" Raihan shouted excitedly from commentary.

Leon ordered:

"Dragapult, fly up!"

Dragapult was faster than most pseudo-legendaries.

It tried to bolt—

BAM!!

Dragonite smashed into Dragapult's back!

Though the damage wasn't devastating (both were in motion), Dragapult used the hit's momentum to widen the distance.

Meanwhile, the Dreepy missiles finally caught up.

BOOM! BOOM!

They hit Dragonite, but thanks to Dragonite's Multiscale ability, the damage was minor.

Leon's eyes sharpened.

"Keep your distance—Dragon Darts again!"

Swoosh swoosh—

Two more Dreepy launched.

Dragapult kept flying, maintaining maximum distance from Dragonite.

Dragonite was forced to dodge deftly, a surprisingly agile feat for such a large body.

It chased.

Meanwhile:

Dragapult leading.

Dragonite chasing.

Dreepy chasing Dragonite.

A bizarre high-speed game of cat and mouse unfolded.

Raihan facepalmed.

"Here it is—Leon's famous 'Kiting Dragon' tactic: speed advantage, stay mobile, wear them down."

Among the spectators, nobody understood Leon better than Raihan.

Dragapult, boosted by Dragon Dance, was fast.

It kept Dragging Darts flying.

But—

Dragapult glanced behind.

Huh?

Why isn't this Dragonite slowing down?

It's not even tired?

Dragapult was puzzled.

It didn't know—

Dragonite loved this.

At home, Johan's Gengar had to drag Dragonite on massive flights just to burn its endless energy.

This chase?

Child's play.

Johan's gaze sharpened.

You think you're "wandering the skies"?

No.

You don't understand what it means to be a "Wandering Dragon."

"Dragonite!"

He adjusted his monocle dramatically.

"Extreme Speed!"

Dragonite's acceleration skyrocketed.

Raihan started explaining:

"Extreme Speed is fast, but it's a Normal-type move.

Against a Ghost/Dragon like Dragapult, it won't work—"

But—

Steven shook his head in the stands.

"That's no ordinary Extreme Speed."

Watch carefully.

"Look!"

someone gasped.

Dragonite's usual whitish speed trails...

turned green!

The swirling energy—

took the shape of an arrow.

An arrow?!

Dragon Darts!?

Raihan was stunned.

"Wait—did Dragonite learn Dragon Darts by watching Dreepy?"

"No," Cynthia said calmly.

"You're looking at something different."

BOOM!!

Dragonite smashed into Dragapult head-on, ignoring type immunities.

Dragapult reeled in pain.

The audience erupted in shock.

"Why can Normal-type Extreme Speed hit Dragapult?!"

"That's not logical—oh wait, it's Johan. Never mind."

"If Dragapult can't even out-speed it, how can it win?"

"Wandering Dragon?

No.

THIS is the true 'Wandering Dragon!'"

Some Hoenn natives, those with good memories, remembered a long-deleted video...

A clip of Johan riding atop Rayquaza, performing a mystical finishing move.

"I swear... that move looks just like Rayquaza's Dragon Ascent!"

Taught by a certain legendary friend.

Don't ask.

Just accept.

# 561: I’ll Take on All Ten!

Leon's kite-flying tactic with Dragapult failed. When it came to raw speed, Dragonite was hardly inferior to Dragapult.

But with Extreme Speed — or rather, a modified form of it — Dragonite completely overwhelmed Dragapult in terms of speed.

Finally:

"Dragapult is unable to battle!"

The referee announced loudly, his voice filled with excitement.

He felt that today's battlefield was so safe — nothing like the danger his colleagues had warned him about. Easy day at work!

"Good job, Dragapult."

Leon recalled it gently back into its Poké Ball.

Thud—

Meanwhile, on the big screen, the Poké Balls representing Inteleon and Dragapult dimmed next to Leon's portrait — two Pokémon down!

Although Johan's side still had Ceruledge, Gengar, and Dragonite active, with all three Poké Balls still lit up, both Ceruledge and Dragonite had taken considerable damage.

Ceruledge from the fierce clashes with Cinderace.

Dragonite from the intense duel with Dragapult.

Johan had the advantage, but it wasn't as overwhelming as it might have seemed.

Leon's fighting spirit remained high.

He still had a big chance.

After a brief consideration, Leon tossed his fourth Poké Ball.

At the same time, Johan also recalled Dragonite.

It had done more than enough.

BOOM BOOM—

With heavy drumbeats, a massive, dark brown gorilla-like Pokémon with green leafy patches on its head, wrists, and waist appeared on the field.

Rillaboom!

Rillaboom grabbed what looked like a wooden stake, which instantly expanded into a massive drum.

It grabbed two wooden drumsticks, and bam-bam-bam —

the powerful drumbeats echoed.

Bam bam bam—

The rhythmic beats spread out, causing a lush green grassy terrain to overgrow the battlefield.

Grassy Terrain!

Leon's Rillaboom clearly had the Grassy Surge ability.

[Grassy Surge: Upon entering battle, the Pokémon creates a Grassy Terrain.]

This ability was similar to the Psychic Surge that Johan's Indeedee had — except this one summoned grass, not psychic fields.

In the Pokémon world, there were abilities to summon weather — hail, sandstorms, harsh sunlight —

and there were abilities to summon terrains — grassy, psychic, misty, etc.

These features expanded a Pokémon's natural strengths significantly.

"Rillaboom, the Drummer Pokémon, Grass type. Known for steady personalities and strong emotional control. Their drumbeats can be loud enough to shake the ground—Rotom!"

Compared to Leon's previous Pokémon, Johan was much more familiar with Rillaboom, a frequent powerhouse in Pokémon Battle tournaments.

Bam bam bam—

Rillaboom's drumming echoed across the field.

Gengar was even bouncing along to the beat, seemingly appreciating its musical skills.

After confirming Leon's fourth Pokémon, Johan, still maintaining the tactical advantage of sending out Pokémon after seeing Leon's pick, threw out his fourth Poké Ball.

"Oowoo~~"

Amid the lush greenery, snowflakes began to fall, gradually blanketing the field.

Graceful, ethereal — a mystical figure danced between ice and grass.

Ninetales!

"As expected — Ice Ninetales versus Grassy Terrain!"

Commentator Raihan was back on form.

Earlier, when Dragonite's Extreme Speed broke logic, he was left speechless.

But now — he could finally analyze again.

Ice attacks were super effective against Grass types.

However, Rillaboom wasn't helpless either; Grass moves could hit Ice Ninetales neutrally.

This would be a proper duel.

Also, while Johan had the advantage of counter-picking, Leon now held the first move advantage.

Sure enough—

"Rillaboom, Fake Out!"

Bam bam bam—

With an urgent rhythm, two thick brown vines shot up from the grassy terrain, slapping at Ninetales.

Using Fake Out's priority, the vines smacked Ninetales before it could react!

Wham! Wham!

Hit successful!

"Rillaboom, Taunt!"

Leon immediately followed up.

Bam bam—

Rillaboom pounded the drum, then cocked a finger at Ninetales provocatively.

Taunt —

prevented the opponent from using status moves for a time, blocking abilities like Aurora Veil or Nasty Plot.

"Leon is very cautious,"

Raihan observed.

Normally, after Fake Out induced flinching, you'd want to attack again immediately.

But Leon prioritized Taunt instead, fearing Johan's tricks.

Against Johan, caution was justified.

Sure enough—

Even before Taunt fully landed, Ninetales shook off the flinch.

A Pokémon raised by a professional Pokémon psychologist wouldn't have weak mental fortitude.

Leon's caution had paid off.

"Tch."

Johan clicked his tongue slightly.

Very cautious indeed.

"Rillaboom, Drum Beating!"

Leon pressed the advantage.

Bam bam bam—

Two more vines lashed out toward Ninetales.

Even though Rillaboom focused on physical offense, using vines via grassy terrain made it unpredictable.

"Evade!"

Johan said calmly.

"Oowoo~~"

Ninetales, although Taunted, moved with lithe elegance, dodging the slashing vines easily.

"Blizzard!"

WHOOOSH—

Arctic winds surged.

What had been gentle snow now transformed into a blizzard of sharp ice blades!

Rillaboom tried to Grassy Glide across the field to avoid the onslaught.

But—

"Oowoo!!"

With a proud howl, Ninetales amplified the blizzard, expanding it to cover the entire field.

Without the protection of Aurora Veil, Ninetales felt somewhat vulnerable—

thus, it unleashed everything.

The blizzard's power—

"Incredible! So strong!"

Raihan exclaimed.

Facing Johan in battle was way different than just watching from commentary.

The Blizzard now was even more terrifying than what he had faced.

Crack crack crack—

Frost began creeping up Rillaboom's body.

Still, it stubbornly pounded its drum.

Wham! Wham!

The vines struck at a Ninetales—

only for the target to collapse into snow!

"A Substitute? No! A Snowman!"

Leon immediately realized.

Taunted Ninetales couldn't use Substitute —

but building snowmen as decoys using Blizzard?

Allowed.

"Rillaboom, Boomburst!"

BOOM BOOM BOOM—

The drumbeats grew thunderous.

A massive sonic wave blasted outward, shaking off the ice from Rillaboom's body,

scattering all the snowmen and clearing the field!

"Battle Cry!"

"ROAR!!"

[Battle Cry: Lowers the opponent's Attack and Special Attack.]

Johan breathed a faint puff of white mist.

"Ninetales—Blizzard again."

WHOOOSH—

The second Blizzard was even stronger, aided subtly by psychic power.

"It's using Psychic energy?!"

Caitlin gasped from the spectator stands.

Ninetales wasn't just controlling snow.

It was directing it with psychic precision!

Frost mist shrouded Ninetales, its pale blue eyes shimmering.

Not safe.

Better use 50% strength.

A layer of fine mist surrounded Ninetales' body.

"Am I seeing things?!"

someone in the stands cried.

"The soundwaves... FROZE?!"

another shouted.

There, midair—

The ripples of Rillaboom's Battle Cry were trapped in a sheet of ice!

Even Leon and Rillaboom were stunned.

Regardless of whether they understood it,

The double Blizzard overwhelmed the field!

"Rillaboom, Wood Hammer! Smash through the ice!"

Bam bam bam—

Huge vines slammed around furiously.

Leon chose a direct brawl.

Either freeze Rillaboom entirely, or smash through to Ninetales.

But—

Johan smiled faintly.

"Ninetales, Ice Shard."

Amid the freezing storm, a sharp chunk of ice shot from Ninetales' tail.

WHAM!!

Like snapping a frozen spring water bottle,

Rillaboom was instantly covered in crystal ice!

"Rillaboom is unable to battle!"

The referee announced.

Ninetales had won!

Ninetales paused, sighing softly.

So close to messing up...

Better ask Johan to help me train anti-Taunt responses later...

Thud—

Rillaboom's portrait darkened.

Leon had lost another Pokémon.

"Such a shame,"

Raihan shook his head.

Honestly, Leon and Rillaboom had fought brilliantly.

But Johan's Ice Ninetales was simply on another level.

Leon silently recalled Rillaboom.

Now the disadvantage was stark.

Most importantly—

Johan still held the counter-pick advantage.

That was suffocating.

Leon had to change the tempo!

Huff.

Leon exhaled, slapping his cheeks lightly.

There was still hope!

"Go, Cinderace!"

Once more, Cinderace returned to the field.

Fwoosh!!

The intense flames around it weakened the snowstorm dramatically.

Johan's eyes flashed.

"Gengar."

"Ka-kaah?"

Me again?

Gengar stepped up eagerly.

At the same time, Ninetales nimbly leaped back to the sidelines, tapping Gengar's hand with her tail.

Clap!

"Ka-kaah,"

Gengar blew warm breath onto his icy hand,

then smirked mischievously at Cinderace.

Cinderace vs. Gengar?

"Cinderace, Quick Attack to close in!"

Leon called.

ZOOM—

Cinderace's hair turned white; it zipped toward Gengar.

Its type changed to Normal.

Normal > Ghost?

Was Leon planning something?

"U-turn!"

Leon shouted as Cinderace neared.

Ah—

Quick Attack to reposition,

then U-turn to escape and reverse the tempo!

Smart!

However—

Johan smiled faintly.

"Gengar, Skill Swap!"

"Ka-kaah!"

A dark tendril from Gengar's shadow latched onto Cinderace.

Abilities swapped.

Now:

Cinderace had Cursed Body.

Gengar had Libero!

Immediately afterward, Cinderace disappeared back into its Poké Ball.

On the field:

Johan and Gengar wore near-identical mischievous smirks.

Libero acquired.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar danced joyfully across the battlefield.

This was a "snatch the treasure" moment!

Gengar even donned sunglasses dramatically:

"Ka-kaah-ka-kaah~! (▼⊿▼)"

This time, I'll take on all TEN of you!

# 562: The Final Showdown!

Leon finally managed to seize back the advantage of counter-switching his Pokémon.

But truthfully—

There weren't many options left for Leon.

Aside from Cinderace, he only had two Pokémon remaining.

Still, from Leon's perspective, as long as he could deny Johan the counter-picking advantage, it was a win.

Because Johan's familiarity with Pokémon was too thorough, and he was a master at exploiting type matchups.

Letting him have counter-switching was just too dangerous.

But despite all the maneuvering, even though Leon successfully switched out Cinderace...

Gengar now possessed the Libero ability.

As Cinderace's trainer, Leon knew exactly how powerful Libero was, how it could turn the tide of battle.

And worse—

It was now in the hands of a Pokémon as tricky as Gengar.

"But..."

Leon's expression turned grave.

"Gengar isn't the only tricky Pokémon out there."

"Go, Mr. Rime!"

"The fifth Pokémon from Leon: Mr. Rime!"

The announcer shouted excitedly.

A Pokémon, dressed like a dapper gentleman, leaning on an icy cane, complete with a Charlie Chaplin mustache and tap shoes, slid onto the battlefield.

Mr. Rime, a Galar-region evolution of Mr. Mime —

a Pokémon unique to Galar.

In other regions, Mr. Mime could not evolve.

"Hum~"

Because of the earlier influence of Ninetales, the field was still covered in ice and snow —

essentially turning the battlefield into an icy stage.

And this—

gave Mr. Rime his stage.

He skated smoothly across the field, turning the ground he touched into slick ice.

On one side, Gengar, wearing shades, was bouncing and taunting — "I'll take on ten!"

On the other side, Mr. Rime tap-danced across the ice, twirling his cane like a circus act.

Anyone watching might have thought they were at a circus show.

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar lifted his shades slightly and squinted at the figure skating Mr. Rime.

Rival detected.

A spotlight-stealer!

Gengar licked his lips, dangerous red glint in his eyes.

"Mr. Rime, the Comedian Pokémon, Ice and Psychic type.

A master of tap dancing and comedy performances—Rotom!"

Rotom faithfully delivered the info —

but against Johan's instinctive reading, it didn't add much.

Johan looked at Gengar with a strange expression.

No one understood Gengar better than Johan.

One scratch of its backside and he knew whether it was plotting to steal food.

One look at its face and he knew what it was thinking.

And now—

Gengar was serious!

For the first time in the entire Youth Masters World Championship,

Gengar had gotten serious.

Leon probably never imagined:

The Pokémon he sent to deal with Gengar…

had fired up Gengar instead.

The battle resumed.

"Gengar, Dark Pulse!"

Johan issued the first command.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar narrowed his eyes, rapidly rubbing his hands together.

A wave of concentrated malicious energy formed between them, crackling ominously.

At the same time,

thanks to Libero,

Gengar's type changed to Dark!

A Dark-type Gengar!

Perfect against Mr. Rime's Ice/Psychic typing.

Dark moves were immune to Psychic attacks and weren't weak to Ice either.

Gengar also had many Dark-type moves in his arsenal.

And being Dark-type now—

meant Taunt wouldn't hinder him either!

Mr. Rime twirled his cane rapidly, conjuring a transparent Light Screen to block the Dark Pulse.

Meanwhile—

"Mr. Rime, Role Play!"

Leon ordered.

"Hum—"

Mr. Rime's face briefly morphed into Gengar's likeness.

Its ability copied Libero too!

Although Role Play was a Psychic move, it could bypass type interactions.

However—

Gengar gaped, turning to Johan.

"Ka-kaah! (Д)σ"

"He's copying me?!"

Normally it was Gengar imitating others.

This was the first time someone copied Gengar!

Johan twitched at the corner of his mouth.

What could he even say?

At the moment Mr. Rime finished copying Libero,

Gengar's Dark Pulse shattered the Light Screen,

continuing its surge toward Mr. Rime.

Leon swung his arm urgently.

"Mr. Rime, Misty Terrain!"

Before the strange mist could fully expand,

Mr. Rime, thanks to Libero,

shifted into Fairy type.

Fairy types resist Dark attacks!

BOOM!

Mr. Rime was blasted back —

but the damage was greatly reduced.

The pink mist spread, covering the battlefield.

"Ugh—my head can't keep up anymore,"

moaned a spectator.

"It's simple!"

someone else explained.

"Gengar became Dark type, Mr. Rime became Fairy type,

both now have Libero,

and Libero only applies when using the first move!"

Simple?

Yeah, right.

Everyone's brains were twisted into knots.

Misty Terrain expanded, suppressing statuses like poisoning, paralysis, and confusion.

Another blow to Gengar.

Gengar was furious.

He had been enjoying his Libero tricks—

and now Mr. Rime copied it.

And now Misty Terrain shut down his debuffs?

Rage.

Pure, bubbling rage.

Johan adjusted his monocle.

Can't overwrite the terrain?

Who said we needed to?

"Gengar," he said quietly.

"Maximum power — Sludge Wave."

"Ka-kaah!!"

Gengar inhaled deeply—

his body swelling grotesquely.

THOOM!

A filthy, viscous tidal wave of sludge erupted,

spreading outward in all directions.

Snow, ice, leftover rocks—

everything corroded away into stinking, toxic sludge.

Leon's face changed instantly.

Such intense poison!

He knew Gengar had scary Ghost powers—

but this?

This toxic power hadn't been revealed until now!

"Mr. Rime, Light Screen! Watch out for poison!"

Even if Misty Terrain blocked status effects,

getting directly hit by such sludge still hurt.

Mr. Rime barely raised another Light Screen.

But—

the sludge tsunami was relentless.

The arena's protective barriers even hissed and sizzled.

Spectators started pulling their feet back instinctively.

Staff rushed to reinforce the shields.

But inside the field—

Mr. Rime's Light Screen was melting.

Hissing.

Crackling.

Breaking.

"唔!!"

Mr. Rime frantically raised a second barrier.

But the corrosion was relentless.

Purple toxic mist began rising around Mr. Rime.

Mr. Rime was poisoned!

Wait—

wasn't there Misty Terrain?!

"Look!"

someone cried out.

"The terrain disappeared!"

How?

Sludge Wave—

at this scale,

was powerful enough to sweep away terrain effects.

In short:

Mr. Rime: poisoned.

Misty Terrain: gone.

Field: devastated, full of pits and holes.

"Ka-kaah!!"

Gengar bounced gleefully.

"My domain again!"

Leon grimaced.

When things went south against Johan,

it really went south.

"Mr. Rime, Safeguard!"

Maybe prevent further poison?

But—

Gengar wasn't going to let him.

Dark energy boiled from Gengar.

Not Shadow Ball this time—

but a deeper darkness:

"Dark Void!"

WHOOOOM—

The black orb enveloped Mr. Rime—

before it could finish Safeguard!

THUD—

Mr. Rime collapsed, deeply asleep.

Gengar grinned evilly, rubbing his hands.

Normally he avoided sleep attacks—

too cruel.

Johan discouraged it.

But today—

Gengar was pissed.

And Mr. Rime was going to pay.

Thud—

Mr. Rime's portrait on the scoreboard dimmed.

"Mr. Rime is unable to battle!"

the trembling referee announced.

Leon had lost his fourth Pokémon.

Now—

Aside from Cinderace, only one remained.

The real threat was coming.

Johan murmured:

"The real monster appears."

Infernape could steamroll three of Cynthia's Pokémon when going berserk.

Charizard, compared to that, was even stronger.

Their bond, no less deep.

Even with a numbers advantage,

Johan didn't dare relax.

A careless mistake could let Leon's Charizard sweep his whole team!

"Ka-kaah!!"

Gengar thumped his belly.

"I'm angry! I'm not stepping down!"

He was still fired up to fight all ten!

Leon clutched his final Poké Ball.

Eyes gleaming with fiery determination.

"No more hesitation."

"Go—my partner! Charizard!"

"RAAARRGH!!"

A blast of heat erupted as Charizard appeared,

its wings stirring fierce hot winds.

Gengar squinted against the gusts.

"Ka-kaah…"

So strong.

He turned to Johan, pointing at Charizard:

"Ka-kaah?"

"Can we count this one as nine Pokémon?"

Johan blinked—

then chuckled and nodded.

"Sure."

It wasn't just any Pokémon.

It was the ace.

A true clash of titans:

Gengar vs Charizard.

Ace vs Ace.

Bring it on.

# 563: VS Leon’s Charizard!

"Leon's Charizard," Raihan said gravely.

As Leon's greatest rival in Galar, he knew Charizard's strength better than anyone—and understood the bond Leon shared with it.

Their opponent: Johan and his Gengar.

Though Leon only had Charizard and a battered Cinderace remaining…

Johan still had a fatigued Ceruledge and Dragonite, as well as a battle-ready Gengar, Ninetales, and two unused Pokémon.

And yet—

With Charizard's entrance, Leon's confidence surged, as if he now held the upper hand.

Some Trainers are just like that.

Take Flint, for example. Even after losing to Cynthia 149 times, the 150th time, he'd still believe he could win with Infernape.

Leon was even more formidable.

While Flint was a pure emotion-driven Trainer—fiery, passionate—

Leon combined emotional spirit and cold, calculated tactics.

Even in the games, his team setups, movesets, and item selections all reflected a meta strategist.

In that sense… he and Johan were very much alike.

Just look at his tactical Mr. Rime, dual-wielding Inteleon, Libero Cinderace, Dragon Tail Dragapult—

A fellow quality player.

Which is why Johan wasn't taking Charizard lightly.

No Dynamaxing allowed here—but Leon might pull out a Mega Charizard X or Y, or even something sneakier like Solar Power.

In the original story, Leon's Charizard went toe-to-toe with Lance's Dynamaxed Gyarados—type advantages be damned.

As the audience erupted in cheers, the final showdown began!

"Charizard, Scorching Sands!"

"RAAARGH!"

Charizard spread its wings and howled. Scalding sand erupted around it, spiraling into a furnace-like storm rushing toward Gengar.

Johan wanted to scream—

Give my Gengar its Levitate back!

Without Levitate, Gengar could be hit by Ground-type moves.

Though now a Dark-type thanks to Libero, Gengar wasn't weak to Ground—

but Scorching Sands could burn, and that was annoying.

"Substitute, Thunder Wave!"

Ka-kaah!

Gengar split from his own shadow, leaving behind a decoy to soak up the sandstorm's fury.

Then, sliding through the shadows, Gengar popped up behind Charizard and zapped it with a golden wave of static.

Leon didn't even need to shout.

Charizard, instinctively synced with its Trainer, flared up—its tail blazing hotter to burn away the interference.

Now Gengar and Charizard faced off at close range.

Their stances screamed "Clash Incoming."

Their Trainers shouted simultaneously:

"Ability Swap!"

"Brick Break!"

ZAP!

A flash of energy swapped their Abilities.

Gengar now had Solar Power.

Charizard had Libero.

BAM!

Charizard's Brick Break, now a Fighting-type attack, punched straight through the illusion.

Everyone: "???"

Raihan explained excitedly:

"Johan's signature move—Preemptive Reading!

He predicted Leon would use a Fighting move to counter Gengar's Dark form.

So Gengar switched back to Ghost/Poison—immune to Fighting!"

And with Charizard now a Fighting-type due to Libero…

He couldn't touch Gengar.

Crowd:

"Holy crap—this man really made Libero into a weapon!"

"Gengar: Thanks! Your ability is awesome!"

"Isn't this just like how he used Raihan's weather against him?"

Raihan: "Hey!"

Even Leon and Charizard were stunned.

That was a complete tactical checkmate.

Leon bit his lip, then smiled wide.

"No wonder you're Johan."

He raised his voice:

"But now begins the final round!"

"Charizard!"

"RAAARGH!!"

Flames roared higher, wind howled across the field.

Wait—

Johan blinked.

No way…

Was Leon about to—?

Leon pulled out a Keystone, glowing in rainbow hues.

Charizard's tail fire dimmed… revealing a matching Mega Stone.

Mega Evolution.

Leon had hidden the Mega Stone in Charizard's tail flame.

Johan:

"Oh you sneaky son of a—"

This was a full-gear world. No regional restriction nonsense.

Of course Leon had Mega Evolution tucked away.

Mega Charizard Y!

Rather than X (Fire/Dragon), Leon's stone evolved Charizard into Y—retaining Fire/Flying but buffing Special Attack and Special Defense.

Most important?

Mega Evolution overwrote the Libero swap.

Charizard now had Drought—summoning harsh sunlight!

Audience:

"He Mega Evolved!"

"Wait—why does Charizard get two Mega forms?!"

"This is nuts!!"

On the battlefield:

Gengar popped his shades back on.

Too sunny otherwise.

"Charizard, Flamethrower!"

Leon was all in.

WHOOSH!

Charizard launched into the air, sunlight amplifying the fire.

A molten stream of flame poured from above.

Johan squinted, considering Mega evolving in response.

But even then, the blast couldn't be dodged.

Better to go stealth—

"Gengar, Phantom Force!"

Gengar dove into the shadows.

Audience: "Mega vs Mega when?!"

Leon: "Watch your back!"

Mega Charizard Y spun around—

ZIP!

From behind, Gengar clawed out of the void.

"Ability Swap!"

One more time, their Abilities switched—again.

Now Gengar had Drought.

Mega Charizard Y… didn't.

Audience: "Wait. What was the point of that?"

Johan smiled faintly.

"Gengar, turn it off."

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar smirked and reached out.

Click.

Sunlight vanished.

Audience: "?!???"

Leon: "???"

Charizard: "???"

You can turn off the weather??

Yes—because in this real-world Pokémon battle, abilities like weather weren't always "always-on."

Gengar had practiced stealing and toggling Abilities constantly with his team.

Sunlight: gone.

Leon recovered.

"Charizard, Shadow Claw!"

Even a special attacker like Charizard could hit hard physically.

"Gengar, Thunder!"

Lightning crackled—not from the sky, but from Gengar's hands.

They clashed—mid-air.

Shadow Claw met crackling fists.

BOOM!

Both reeled back.

Charizard—paralyzed.

But even that, he burned away with sheer willpower.

Flamethrower again!

"Shadow Ball!"

Fire and darkness exploded.

The crowd roared.

"Scorching Sands!"

"Sludge Wave!"

Mud and filth met scorching heat—sizzling, stinking clouds rose.

Leon shouted joyfully:

"Yes! This is it! This is what I love—battle, partnership, the thrill!"

Winning didn't matter.

This feeling did.

Johan understood.

But he also knew:

For Gengar, direct slugfests weren't ideal.

Gengar's true strength was deception.

Still—

They had to match fire with fire.

More collisions.

"Flamethrower!"

"Shadow Ball!"

"Dazzling Fire vs Dark Meteor."

Finally—

Gengar compressed a massive Shadow Ball—enhanced with mental focus.

Threw it.

Leon: "Block it—Scorching Sands!"

Charizard tried to knock it aside—

Too late.

BOOM!

Charizard slammed into the dirt.

Raihan: "Wait—that wasn't normal. That was physical impact?"

A hybrid—psychic technique layered onto a special move, like a spiritual armor-break.

Even Gengar's growing.

"Don't give up, Charizard!"

Charizard rose.

Gengar gave him a nod.

"Ka-kaah."

You've earned my respect.

Only three Pokémon had.

Garchomp. Metagross. Now Charizard.

"One more! Flamethrower!!"

"Shadow Ball—one last time!"

Flame met void—

BOOM!!!

Shockwaves rippled. Dust clouded everything.

Everyone held their breath.

As the smoke cleared—

Charizard had de-Mega evolved, lying motionless.

Gengar—panting, sitting on rubble, popped his shades on.

"Ka-kaah~ (_)"

ROAR!!!

The crowd erupted.

Cheers for both warriors.

"Ch-Charizard is unable to battle!!"

Though Leon still had Cinderace, the match was over.

Johan had won.

Leon bowed his head.

Then smiled.

"I lost, Mr. Johan."

Johan exhaled.

Next time, he'd make sure Gengar didn't go off-script.

Leon looked at Gengar, still soaking in applause.

Then at Johan's grin.

He'd train harder. Next time—he'd win.

From the stands, Red and Green sat stunned.

But in each other's eyes—

Resolve.

Someday, they'd battle on this level too.

# 564: I’ve Made It!

"Mr. Johan, you've defeated Champion Leon and advanced to the semifinals. Is there anything you'd like to say to everyone?"

After the match, as arranged by the tournament organizers, a press conference was held. Journalists gathered to interview Johan and Leon.

One reporter from Galar asked earnestly.

"Ummm..." Johan paused slightly before replying, "Leon's Charizard… was really strong."

He turned to look at Gengar sitting beside him, wearing sunglasses and acting all cool.

"Ka-kaah!"

Gengar nodded shortly, adjusting its shades in approval.

The mischievous and rowdy Gengar on the battlefield was now the image of silent swagger—fresh off a satisfying win.

The journalist's eyes lit up at the response, but before she could follow up, Johan added:

"If anyone plans to fight Leon's Charizard, I suggest not using Water-, Rock-, or Ground-type Pokémon."

"Huh?" The Galar reporter blinked. "Why not?"

"Because it uses G-Max Grass."

Galar reporter: "???"

Leon, nearby: "…"

He didn't even use Gigantamax. How did Johan know that?

Then again… a Fire-type Charizard with G-Max Grass does make tactical sense. Like how Raihan always uses Sandstorm strategies against Leon—never works.

"Ahem." A reporter from Hoenn suppressed a laugh and turned to Leon.

"Leon, how would you evaluate Mr. Johan?"

Leon didn't hesitate: "He's really strong. Especially his Gengar."

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar tilted its shades at Leon.

"Anything else?" the Hoenn reporter asked again.

Leon nodded seriously. "Also Ninetales, Dragonite, Ceruledge, Tinkaton, and Urshifu…"

Hold on.

Leon hadn't even battled Tinkaton or Urshifu.

And that wasn't what the reporter meant…

"Mr. Johan, you've now defeated all three major Galar Trainers: Nessa, Raihan, and Leon. Any comments?"

A new voice asked.

Hmm?

Who was trying to sow discord between him and the Galar League?

Johan followed the voice… A reporter wearing a Kalos TV station badge.

He glanced at Gengar.

Remember this station.

Gengar tugged its shades down, revealing a crimson gleam in its eye. It licked its lips and nodded slightly.

"Ka-kaah~"

That guy's definitely wetting the bed tonight.

Johan cleared his throat. "Ahem. You all know—I get along great with Galar Trainers."

"Master Mustard is like a mentor. Leon's practically a fellow disciple. Champion Peony is a good friend. His daughter is my student. Magnolia's granddaughter—also my student…"

The Kalos reporter started sweating.

Wait… does Johan own the Galar League?

Leon remained silent, thinking.

If Johan is Mustard's student… that makes me his peer. If Peony's his friend, then Peony's my friend too.

Wait—

I'm peers with Champion Peony! That means I outrank Steven and the rest by a generation!

Nice!

—

After the interview, before the next match, Johan and Leon chatted.

"Still didn't get to see your Eevee…" Leon said wistfully.

Johan winced.

Since you're so sincere…

"Next time I'm in Galar, I'll bring it."

"Deal!" Leon beamed.

Then, sincerely:

"But that match taught me and my Pokémon a lot."

After all, both Leon and Johan are quality players—with bond-based tactics as their backbone.

Leon had learned a lot from Johan's gameplay during the match.

Johan chuckled awkwardly.

Just don't tell anyone I taught you.

Leon clenched his fist. "Now I'm even more confident for my next tournament!"

"Next tournament?" Johan blinked.

"Oh—Galar's Champion Cup."

The Champion Cup.

Unique to Galar. It has two formats:

One is similar to other regions' League tournaments—badge collection, knockout style. Leon had won this at age ten and caught Rose's attention.

The other is Galar's version of a Champion Path—since Galar has no Elite Four. It's the official Champion's League.

Currently, Champion Peony held that title.

But Leon lowered his voice:

"Chairman Rose has confirmed—Champion Peony has stepped down."

Stepped down?

And it's coming from Rose?

Even though Rose and Peony are brothers, their relationship was…

Still.

If Peony has stepped down and Leon's entering the Champion League—

That means Leon's about to become Galar's official Champion.

Our generation is finally getting its first Champion.

"Go for it." Johan patted his shoulder seriously.

Sure, Leon's current strength might not quite be Champion-level yet. At best, he was about equal with Johan—maybe an Elite Four-tier at most.

But with Rose's backing and Galar's Dynamax advantage—

It wasn't out of reach.

Plus, Leon was only getting stronger.

"I'll win for sure!" Leon grinned. "The Champion Cup isn't for a while though. I'll be training with my teacher until then."

"Make sure to win it!"

Johan smiled as he imagined a future where his friends were all champions:

Galar's Champion Leon, Hoenn's Steven, Sinnoh's Cynthia, Johto's Lance, Kalos's Diantha…

The Pokémon world would basically be his turf.

"You guys better not slack off!"

—

That afternoon, the second quarterfinal began, met with roaring cheers.

Steven from Hoenn vs Diantha from Kalos.

Johan, invited to commentate thanks to his match being done and his deep familiarity with Steven, joined the broadcast.

"Let's go, Aggron!" Steven declared with poise—sending out his iconic "Wall" Pokémon.

"Your turn, Hawlucha!"

Diantha responded with her Flying/Fighting-type striker.

On paper, Aggron was in a terrible spot—quadruple weak to Fighting.

But in real battle, things weren't that simple.

In the games, stats are capped. IVs, EVs, base stats decide power.

But in the real world, Pokémon had no theoretical cap—only what you could train.

Steven was one of the few Trainers willing to pay any price.

With Johan's training, Steven's Aggron had absurd physical defense. And Hawlucha was a physical attacker.

"Hawlucha, High Jump Kick!"

"Aggron, Ice Punch!"

WHAM!!

Hawlucha landed a sliding kick on Aggron's leg, nearly toppling it—

But Aggron maintained balance and countered with a brutal Ice Punch.

The fight was on.

—

In the end, the result shocked everyone.

"Hawlucha, Close Combat!"

"Aggron, Heavy Slam!"

CRASH!!

Both Pokémon fell simultaneously.

A double knockout.

Commentary:

"Steven has the upper hand now…"

"Huh? Didn't they both faint?"

"Is that biased commentary?!"

But Johan explained:

"Hawlucha was Diantha's physical sweeper. Aggron, while important, was mainly there to stop physical attackers."

"That's why Steven didn't switch out, even with a 4x weakness to Fighting."

"Steven has replacements. Diantha? That was probably her only true answer to his whole team."

Makes sense!

Diantha's known Pokémon included: Gardevoir, Goodra, Tyrantrum, Aurorus, Gourgeist.

All weak to Steel-types.

Steven, though a Steel specialist, also used Rock and Ground Pokémon.

Fighting beats Steel and Rock—but with Hawlucha gone, Diantha lost her edge.

And just as Johan predicted…

After Hawlucha fainted, Steven controlled the match's rhythm.

Diantha's Goodra did great—taking two KOs…

But in the final clash:

Mega Metagross vs Mega Gardevoir

Meteor Mash smashed Gardevoir into the dirt.

Steven advanced to the semifinals. Diantha was eliminated.

—

Both matches that day were thrilling for the audience.

Steven was elated; Diantha wasn't discouraged. Like Leon, she gained even more fans.

Supposedly, right after the match, she received dozens of film offers.

Lose the match, win the market.

Johan stared in envy.

So he's the only one actually playing for prize money?

—

That night.

Flint didn't come by to bother him—he was prepping to face Cynthia tomorrow and was deep in training.

But someone else came to see Johan.

Knock knock—

"Gengar, get the door."

Johan was lounging on the sofa, reading Secrets of the Earth.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar, floating mid-air while scrolling through online praise, stuffed the phone into its mouth and crept to the door.

Peeked out.

"KA-KAAH!!"

Gengar recoiled in terror.

"Oowoo?"

Ninetales lifted its head, tails swaying.

Danger?

Johan gave Dragonite a look.

"Draa…"

Dragonite stepped to the door, fist ready.

Three… two… one—OPEN!

"IT'S ME!!"

The moment the door swung open, Dragonite's punch froze inches from Professor Oak's face.

Sweat trickled down Oak's forehead.

Gengar zipped back into Johan's shadow.

That's why Gengar was scared—it was Professor Oak…

Good thing Dragonite hadn't followed through.

But then, Johan noticed who stood beside Oak.

One was his grandson—Green.

The other, wearing a cap and a Trainer outfit…

Red?!

The legendary Red?!

Green and Red bowed slightly.

"Mr. Johan, thank you for having us."

Damn.

I've made it. Even Red calls me "Teacher" now.

# 565: The Semifinal Matchups Are Set

Red—who, in the original story, became the Kanto League Champion after just one year!

Especially in Pokémon Generations, he walked the "Champion's Path" to earn the title.

Green, likewise, also became Champion of the Kanto League within a year. He also walked the Champion's Path.

The only difference was… Green held the title for only thirty minutes.

The one who dethroned him?

Red—called "The Battler" by Professor Oak himself.

Johan had already met Green, but seeing Red was a surprise. Even more so because it was here, in the Unova region.

"Ahem… Hello," Johan recovered from his surprise and greeted them calmly.

He quickly adjusted his emotions.

Sure, in the original lore, Red and Green were legends—but in this world, they were just talented rookies. Things didn't have to follow the same timeline.

Maybe one day Red and Green would be just as amazing—but in one year? Probably not.

After all, even Steven, Cynthia, and Lance hadn't reached Elite Four or Champion level yet.

Besides…

Could anyone really out-weird Boppy?

Johan welcomed the group inside. Professor Oak smiled warmly and introduced Red, then glanced around the room.

He was probably looking for Gengar.

But Gengar was currently hiding in Johan's shadow, trembling.

"Mr. Johan," Red suddenly stood up, his expression serious. "I know it's abrupt, but please accept my challenge!"

…Huh?

Even Green blinked in surprise.

Wait, you're actually serious?

Professor Oak, however, grinned without surprise.

He'd been the same way in his youth.

"That's not really necessary…" Johan smiled and shook his head.

Tempting as it was to flex on a young Red—it just wasn't worth it.

"Please!" Red clutched a Poké Ball in determination.

Seeing his resolve, Green's expression turned serious as well. He gritted his teeth and said:

"If that's the case, then please accept my challenge too, Mr. Johan!"

Great, one Red wasn't enough—now Green too.

Johan turned to Professor Oak.

But the professor simply kept smiling and said nothing.

Fine.

Johan understood that look.

Challenges like this were common in the Pokémon world. Just like in the original series—every time Ash met a Champion or Elite Four member, he'd challenge them on impulse.

Winning didn't matter—battling did.

It was simply part of the culture.

"After the tournament's over," Johan said at last.

"Got it!" Red and Green's eyes lit up with excitement.

"I need special training!" Red jumped up.

"Special training needs a partner," Green stood up too.

The two locked eyes. Sparks flew in the air.

Tch…

Red and Green were probably on par, but Green might have a slight edge right now.

Still, Johan figured it was good to meet them.

After all, both were from Pallet Town.

If any Legendary Pokémon or villainous threats popped up later, they'd make good meatshields—er, helpers.

Even if Red wasn't quite as physically overpowered as Resurrection-Ash, he had tanked an Onix head-on with no damage. That's something.

After introducing them around (and not finding Gengar), Professor Oak took his leave.

"Ka-kaah…"

Gengar sighed in relief from the shadows.

"The world's really becoming more exciting, huh, Gengar?" Johan said with a laugh, lounging on the sofa and petting Ninetales' silky fur.

"Ka-kaah?"

Gengar blinked, not understanding the sudden comment.

Even Johan wasn't sure why he felt that way.

When he first came to this world, all he wanted was to be a simple teacher with one or two Pokémon.

But somewhere along the way, the identity of "Trainer" began to outweigh "Teacher."

His passion for battling slowly overtook his laid-back dreams.

Probably because of his Pokémon.

Each of his partners—whether it was mischievous Gengar, cautious Ninetales, fiercely proud Dragonite—they all embraced battle. Thrived on it.

And that was fine.

Because Johan's love for Pokémon hadn't changed.

He cared for each one of them, for their unique personalities and emotions.

Just like Cynthia always said: when lives collide, something new is born.

Steven's invincible Metagross. Cynthia's regal Garchomp. Flint's fearless Infernape. Leon's brave Charizard.

And in the future…

Ash's unruly Charizard. Master Brother's resilient self. The heart-syncing Greninja.

And Red's future Venusaur. The Snorlax that could stop a high-speed train barehanded.

More and more Pokémon would shine alongside their Trainers.

What a beautiful thing.

The bonds and growth between Trainer and Pokémon would only grow stronger, shining like a diamond.

"Ka-kaah~"

Gengar didn't get what Johan was muttering, but it could feel his joy.

So naturally—it was nap time!

It pulled out a little blanket, curled up on the ceiling, drooling adorably in its sleep.

Today's battle against Leon had taken a lot out of it—after all, it handled Inteleon, Mr. Rime, and Mega Charizard Y all by itself.

Judging by its satisfied grin, it must've seen some glowing praise online.

"Oowoo~"

Ninetales curled up with its head on Johan's lap, sighing contentedly. As long as it was with Johan, nothing else mattered.

With those two resting, others were still wide awake.

"Goka~"

Ceruledge tapped on its newly-repaired armor—courtesy of Tinkaton—and nodded in satisfaction.

It could feel its armor growing stronger with every fix.

Next time, it would shatter even tougher foes!

"Yoji~ Yoji~"

Larvitar mimicked Johan, rocking in its food-bowl-turned-bed while crunching on snacks.

If not for its species, it'd look like a total chubby couch potato.

Tinkaton was still happily hammering away. Raihan's Duraludon had donated loads of metal lately, keeping her busy.

But the most focused?

Urshifu.

Due to recent matchups, Urshifu hadn't appeared in battle.

It never complained—but Johan could tell it was itching for a fight.

"Garruu~"

It practiced punches in mid-air, channeling both Single Strike and Rapid Strike styles.

"Coach Ku," Johan said.

"Garruu?"

Urshifu looked over, puzzled.

"Wanna teach me how to use Aura?" Johan grinned.

"Garruu!"

Urshifu crossed its arms and nodded firmly.

Say no more!

Hidden in its chest fur, a piece of the Fist Plate shimmered red.

—

The next morning, the last quarterfinal match took place: Lance vs Caitlin.

Honestly?

With her psychic powers suppressed, Caitlin didn't stand a chance.

Lance's team were all Flying-types—but most of them had "Dragon" in their names.

And dragon bites aren't just for show.

They chomped their way through Caitlin's team.

Afterward, she flopped into Cynthia's arms, much to Johan's envy.

Cynthia, of course, promised to avenge her.

The happiest person was Lorelei.

"That's just how he is—don't take it personally!"

She played the role of comforting big sister, soothing Caitlin while "exposing" Lance's ruthlessness.

"Anti-Lance League" just gained a new member.

They kept chatting—and Caitlin discovered they both liked cute plushies.

Who'd have guessed ice-cold Lorelei had such a soft spot?

Their friendship blossomed instantly.

—

Meanwhile, the boys were rallying Flint.

"You've still got a shot, man! Beat Cynthia, and we'll make you the boss!"

Lucian, in a maroon suit and reading a book, grinned mischievously.

"Mhm!" Aaron nodded eagerly.

Flint was full of confidence—not sure if it was thanks to last night's training with Lucian or just hype.

"Don't worry. I'll beat her—today!"

Beep—

A phone chimed. Everyone turned to Johan and Gengar, who were putting theirs down.

"Oh, it's nothing," Johan said casually. "Just recording a historic moment."

"Ka-kaah Ka-kaah~"

Gengar nodded and discreetly forwarded the video to Cynthia.

Loyalty first.

Lucian saw through it instantly but didn't call it out.

"Glad someone recorded it," he said smoothly. "I forgot to hit record."

"Really?" Flint scratched his head.

Steven bit his lip, struggling not to laugh.

"Don't hold back," Lance chuckled, patting Steven's shoulder. "Let it out."

Steven coughed twice, side-eyeing Lance.

Bro… You do realize you're the next to fight Cynthia, right?

And you're laughing?

Lance, though, had zero awareness.

Meanwhile, Gengar received Cynthia's reply.

[Eevee Eats Cabbage]: Gengar smile.jpg

—

That afternoon, Cynthia and Flint's match began—heavily anticipated by fans.

"Breaking news! This will be their 149th official battle. They know each other very well!"

The announcer declared.

In the audience—

"Why're you looking at me? This must be Lucian's doing," Johan said sternly.

"Huh?" Lucian blinked.

"Match has started!" Johan quickly changed the subject. "Oh, starting with Gastrodon? Cynthia's serious."

Water/Ground Gastrodon could wipe most of Flint's Fire-types off the map.

But Flint had learned from Leon.

"SolarBeam? Flint's playing tactical now?"

The match was fiery and fun.

Even though Flint had lost to Cynthia 148 times, his passion never faded.

"We won't give up, Infernape!!"

"Ku!!"

Infernape's flames flared wildly, turning the battlefield red.

Blaze activated!

Infernape went beast mode, defeating Cynthia's Lucario and Milotic—

Only to fall to her Garchomp.

A glorious defeat, forever etching Flint and his Infernape into fans' hearts.

—

With Flint's loss, the final four of the Youth Masters World Championship were set:

Lance, Steven, Johan, and Cynthia!

Johan had expected this… but still muttered:

Damn… how are we supposed to win now?

He called Lance "the Dodo" for fun—but never underestimated his strength.

He remembered watching Lance on the news when he first arrived in this world.

That was less than a year ago.

How fast everything had changed.

Steven was his student, sure—but they were more like friends. Johan had helped Steven grow, and Steven helped him, too.

That invincible Shiny Metagross had just as much care and effort poured into it as Gengar.

As for Cynthia…

She was Johan's original sugar momma.

Her Garchomp might be 4x weak to Ice…

But since Cynthia regularly took her baby Gible hiking up Mount Coronet, it had definitely built up resistance.

So yeah… fighting her?

That'd wreck Gengar's already-sensitive stomach.

Johan could only sigh:

"Mewtwo, save me."

After Cynthia's match ended, the semifinals were officially announced:

Johan vs Steven!

Lance vs Cynthia!

Steven looked at Johan, eyes burning.

Finally. A match against his teacher.

----------

# 566: After All, It Is a Legendary Beast!

After another day of rest, the skies were clear and the weather perfect.

Castelia City's Castelia Arena—capable of holding nearly 100,000 spectators—was packed to the brim.

The semifinals of the Youth Masters World Championship were about to begin.

The roaring anticipation of the crowd surged like waves through the stadium, their cheers echoing in unison, brimming with excitement and support for the competitors.

"Mr. Johan versus Steven—a battle of mentor and student!"

Flint, now eliminated from the top eight, sat alongside Cynthia, Lucian, and Aaron.

He didn't look the least bit down about losing. On the contrary, he was enjoying the spectacle with a grin.

Mentor versus student.

A match second only to husband-and-wife battles in thrill—it was bound to be electric.

Flint's mind wandered, recalling some of the "historical tales" Lucian had shared with him—epic master-disciple showdowns, even dramatic "teacher-slaying" clashes.

"I really can't tell who's stronger, Mr. Johan or Steven," Lucian mused aloud.

They all turned to Cynthia.

She was slowly licking an ice cream cone, pretending not to hear them at all.

"Boss—mmph!" Flint's words were cut off as Lucian covered his mouth.

"Don't interrupt her when she's eating ice cream!" Lucian hissed.

"Hmph?"

"You've known her this long—don't you realize she eats ice cream when she's happy, when she's upset, when she's stressed, or just thinking hard?"

Flint blinked.

So… all the time?

Isn't that a bit too frequent?

Lucian continued, "Whatever her mood, if that ice cream falls…"

"What happens?" Flint pried Lucian's hand off.

Lucian stared into the distance with a solemn look. "It was a cold autumn day… the first time I met her…"

"And who do you think will win—Johan or Steven?" a new voice interrupted his story.

They turned.

"Drake of the Elite Four?!"

Why was he in Unova?

"Heard Johan and Steven were facing off. I had no missions at sea, so I just floated over," Drake laughed heartily.

…Floated?

Some people really had too much time.

Lucian, meanwhile, saw a glimpse of the future—Flint's future, and maybe even his own.

Drake smacked his lips.

"Johan, Steven… and Wallace. Sigh. So many good problems to have." He wore the expression of a man "burdened" with exceptional juniors.

If only Bertha from Sinnoh were here—Drake might've shown off even more.

But Lucian and the others understood what he was hinting at.

It was a flex—boasting about Johan and Steven.

Hmph. Whether Johan ultimately joined Hoenn was still up in the air, Lucian thought, sneaking a glance at Cynthia.

Aside from Bertha, he was probably the one who most wanted Johan to join the Sinnoh League.

In Sinnoh, it was policy to rotate younger leaders into League positions—and Lucian knew a reshuffle was coming post-tournament.

Perhaps…

"There he is—Steven!"

The crowd erupted in cheers.

Steven stepped out from the player's tunnel, dressed sharply in a suit, his usual gentle smile unshaken.

He had long awaited this match.

To finally face his teacher—victory wasn't the point anymore.

He simply wanted to show Johan the bond he had built with his Pokémon. To show who he'd become since shaking off his past uncertainties.

"And now, also from Hoenn—Mr. Johan!!"

Johan stepped out in a similarly sharp suit, topped with a modestly-sized fedora and his trademark monocle. All he needed was a cane to complete the look.

"Mr. Johan's so handsome!"

"Steven! No—Johan! I can't decide!"

"Just remember: if there's Johan, choose Johan. If not, go with Steven!"

…

Under the spotlight and roaring applause, Johan and Steven stood on opposite sides of the battlefield.

For the semifinals, the field was no longer random—it was a neutral, standard terrain to minimize environmental factors.

As for who was stronger—no one could say.

But analyzing their teams' typings and possible counters? That was possible.

"Overall, as long as Johan uses Ceruledge and Urshifu wisely, he should have the advantage against Steven," Lorelei noted.

Fire-type Ceruledge posed a real threat to Steven's Steel-types.

However, Steven's team also included Rock- and Ground-types—dangerous for Ceruledge.

Urshifu, being a Fighting-type, was a threat to both Steel- and Rock-types.

So ultimately, the key was how Johan played Urshifu.

"But Steven does have one advantage—Johan's Ninetales will struggle," Lance added with a grin.

Ice/Fairy-type Ninetales was 4x weak to Steel—Steven could easily counter it.

Lorelei didn't miss the jab. She snorted and turned away.

Lance just chuckled to himself. "Old hag."

Glare.

Lorelei whipped around. "Lance! Say that again and you're dead!"

"Later!"

On the field:

"Both competitors, please prepare!" The referee—dressed in armor and riding an Aegislash—signaled.

"Teacher," Steven smiled, clutching a Poké Ball. "I've waited a long time for this."

"Please—don't hold anything back!"

Johan raised an eyebrow.

So you've been hiding your rebellious streak this whole time, huh?

He too pulled out a Poké Ball and nodded.

"Come at me with everything you've got, Steven."

If they were going to fight, they'd fight with pride—Johan had no intention of being overshadowed.

Steven's smile deepened. With a determined look, he hurled his Poké Ball.

"Then I choose—you! Go, Cradily!"

A green, plant-like Pokémon appeared, with yellow markings, crescent eyes, eight pink tentacles around its head, and stubby legs rooted firmly to the ground.

"Cradily, the Barnacle Pokémon. Rock/Grass-type. A recently revived fossil Pokémon—Rotom!"

This was the Lileep Johan had once helped Steven rehabilitate. Now fully evolved into Cradily, it had become one of Steven's core battlers.

This was also the first time Steven used Cradily in the tournament—he hadn't summoned it against Diantha.

A surprise pick, just for Johan?

Well played, Steven. Out to defeat your teacher, huh?

"Gurgle~"

Cradily wobbled happily at the sight of Johan. Far more confident than its timid days as a Lileep.

Even on this massive stage, under countless gazes, it showed no fear.

A sign of growth.

Johan gave a small smile, then tossed his own Poké Ball.

Out came—Urshifu!

"Garruu!!"

Majestic and fierce, Urshifu let out a roar and clashed fist and palm together, unleashing a gust of fighting spirit.

It was finally time!

Urshifu's heart and body were burning with determination.

Typing-wise, both sides had an edge over the other.

Cradily was 2x weak to Fighting; Urshifu was 2x weak to Grass.

But Johan knew Cradily's Ability: Storm Drain!

[Storm Drain]: This Pokémon is immune to Water-type moves. If hit by one, its Special Attack increases by 1 stage.

That mattered.

Because Urshifu's Surging Strikes was a Water-type move—albeit a multi-hit one.

With both sides ready, the referee raised their hand.

"Battle—begin!"

The Youth Masters World Championship Semifinal was on!

"Cradily, Sandstorm!" Steven commanded, seizing the initiative and the weather.

Raihan and Leon had taught everyone one thing:

Never give Johan control.

If you did—you might never get it back.

Sure, your lead might get read and countered—but at least you wouldn't be entirely on the back foot.

"Gurgle~"

Cradily swayed, summoning a swirling sandstorm across the battlefield. The air filled with stinging grit.

If someone looked closely, they'd see Cradily's feet covered in fine, dense roots that dug deep into the field.

Moisture was rapidly drawn from the ground, drying and cracking the soil.

Storm Drain active!

With the dry terrain and Cradily's setup, the sandstorm intensified.

Few might notice—but Johan, who taught Steven this tactic—noticed everything.

He raised an eyebrow.

So you're really pulling this stunt right in front of me?

"Sandstorm? Steven's playing weather too now?"

"But wait… doesn't Johan's Ninetales own weather?"

Lance, today's guest commentator, shared a different perspective:

"No. Johan won't switch in Ninetales. That Sandstorm is a calculated trap—Steven's baiting it. If Johan does switch, Steven'll throw in Metagross or Aggron immediately. He's betting on it."

A Fairy/Ice-type like Ninetales was 4x weak to Steel. If it came out now, the rhythm of the match would swing out of Johan's control.

And Steven knew Johan wouldn't risk that.

Just like Johan knew Steven—Steven knew Johan.

And he was right. Johan didn't switch.

He simply gave the command:

"Urshifu, Swords Dance!"

"Garruu!!"

Even as sand pelted its fur like needles, Urshifu's excitement grew stronger.

It struck a fighting pose—one leg forward, one back—and bounced on the spot.

A metallic, slicing hum filled the air as its aura surged.

Urshifu's eyes sharpened to a deadly gleam.

Switch?

No need!

It hadn't battled in a while.

Did people forget?

After all—it's still a legendary beast.
